```java
        if (processedRepo.existsByDedupKey(k)) return;       // same TX visibility
        orderService.apply(evt);
        processedRepo.save(new ProcessedEvent(k, Instant.now()));
    }
}
```

**Processed-events table:** small, append-only, cleaned up by TTL (days). Add a **unique constraint** on `dedup_key` to make the insert idempotent even under race conditions (catch `DataIntegrityViolationException` on retry).

---

### 4. Idempotency Keys for REST APIs

```java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/payments")
class PaymentController {
    @PostMapping
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.CREATED)
    PaymentResponse charge(@RequestHeader("Idempotency-Key") UUID key,
                            @RequestBody ChargeRequest req) {
        return paymentService.charge(req, key);
    }
}

@Service
class PaymentService {
    @Transactional
    PaymentResponse charge(ChargeRequest req, UUID key) {
        // Check if we already processed this key
        return idempotencyRepo.findByKey(key)
            .map(r -> PaymentResponse.from(r.getPaymentId(), r.getStatus()))  // return cached result
            .orElseGet(() -> {
                Payment p = paymentGateway.charge(req);                        // external call
                idempotencyRepo.save(new IdempotencyRecord(key, p.getId(), p.getStatus(), Instant.now()));
                return PaymentResponse.from(p);
            });
    }
}
```

- The client generates a UUID per logical operation (e.g., "submit payment once") and reuses it on retry.
- Store `(key → result)` in the same DB transaction as the side effect — same-key replay returns the **identical response** (status/body) without re-charging.
- Include **TTL** on `idempotency_record` (cleanup) and a unique index on `key`.

---

### 5. Exactly-Once End-to-End — Outbox + Idempotent Consumers

The combination that achieves **effectively exactly-once** across services/DBs:

```java
// 1. WRITE domain change + outbox event in the SAME local transaction (atomic)
@Transactional
void placeOrder(Order order) {
    orders.save(order);
    outboxRepo.save(new OutboxEvent(
        eventId = UUID.randomUUID().toString(),        // dedup key for consumers
        aggregateType = "Order",
        aggregateId = order.getId(),
        eventType = "OrderPlaced",
        payload = order.json()
    ));                                               // committed together (Q223)
}

// 2. RELAY — publish outbox events (scheduler or Debezium CDC) → broker (at-least-once)
@Scheduled(fixedDelay = 200)
@Transactional
void relay() {
    List<OutboxEvent> pending = outboxRepo.findTop50ByPublishedAtIsNullOrderById();
    for (var e : pending) {
        try {
            kafkaTemplate.send("order-events", e.getAggregateId().toString(), e.toEvent());
            e.setPublishedAt(Instant.now());
            outboxRepo.save(e);                         // mark published
        } catch (Exception ex) {
            // retry next tick — never lose the event
        }
    }
}

// 3. CONSUME — idempotent handler (dedup by eventId) in its own local TX
@KafkaListener(topics = "order-events", groupId = "inventory-service")
public void on(OrderEvent evt, Acknowledgment ack) {
    handler.handle(evt);                                // runs @Transactional with dedup check
    ack.acknowledge();                                  // commit offset after success
}
```

This gives: **no lost events** (outbox) + **no duplicate processing** (consumer dedup by `eventId`) → effectively **exactly-once business effect** even though the broker delivers at-least-once.

---

### 6. Common Pitfalls

| Pitfall | Symptom | Fix |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Dedup key missing or non-unique | Duplicates slip through on retry | Use a stable **eventId** (from outbox) or natural business key + version |
| Dedup check outside the transaction | Race between two threads on the same event | Put `existsByDedupKey` + insert in the **same @Transactional** block; add a **UNIQUE** constraint on `dedup_key` |
| Returning different responses on replay | Client sees inconsistent data on retry | Store the **full response** (status, body, headers) in `idempotency_record` and return it verbatim |
| TTL missing on dedup/idempotency tables | Tables grow unbounded forever | Add `expires_at` + scheduled cleanup job (hourly/daily) |
| Producer idempotence disabled with retries | Kafka produces duplicate records | `enable.idempotence=true`, `acks=all` (Q212) |
| Acking offsets before processing completes | Message lost on crash | Manual ack **after** successful processing (`Acknowledgment.acknowledge()`) |
| External side effect before writing dedup record | Can't dedup if process crashes between call and DB write | Write dedup key/record **inside the same TX** as the business write, or use the **outbox-first** pattern (write outbox first, process side effects after reading outbox) |

### Summary

- **At-least-once + idempotent processing** is the practical path to **effectively exactly-once** semantics across distributed boundaries; true end-to-end exactly-once (cross-system) requires coordination but is rarely necessary if processing is idempotent.
- **Producer idempotence** (`enable.idempotence=true`, Kafka EOS with `transactional.id`) prevents duplicate records from retries (Q212). **Consumer idempotency** uses a **dedup key** (eventId/business key) stored in a `processed_events` table with a UNIQUE constraint inside the same transaction.
- **REST idempotency keys** (`Idempotency-Key` header) return the **cached identical response** on replay and prevent duplicate side effects (charges/refunds).
- **Transactional outbox + idempotent consumers** (Q223) gives **no lost events + no duplicate business effects** — the gold standard for event-driven microservices.

---
